金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 05 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國製造業維持擴張，成本壓力升高：9 月 ISM 製造業調查顯示，美國製造業連續第九個月
位於擴張區間，新訂單與就業分項均見改善，反映接單與用人需求仍有支撐。然而，價格指數
由 8 月的 71.1 升至 9 月的 77.9，能源、金屬及關稅相關成本上升，加上供應商交貨偏慢，企
業面臨更大成本壓力 。 後續須觀察企業能否轉嫁成本 ， 以及通膨黏性對聯準會政策判斷的影響 。
◼ 日本短觀顯示製造業改善、服務業略緩：日本銀行 10 月 1 日公布的 9 月短觀顯示，大型製造

業景氣判斷指數由 6 月的＋22 升至＋24，大型非製造業則由＋37 降至＋35。企業預估 12 月
兩項指數分別回落至＋21 與＋30，顯示現況仍有支撐，後續看法則偏審慎。製造業改善有助
支撐日股獲利預期，但服務業放緩及日銀政策路徑仍須持續觀察。
◼ 台灣製造業活動加速擴張，AI 需求續強：10 月 1 日公布的製造業調查顯示，台灣 9 月製造業
活動擴張速度創 2021 年 8 月以來最快，AI 相關應用需求仍是重要推力。國發會 9 月 29 日公
布的 8 月景氣對策信號續呈紅燈，綜合判斷分數為 41 分，與 7 月持平，領先及同時指標皆續
升。科技供應鏈景氣仍有支撐，但成長動能能否擴及其他產業，仍是觀察後續景氣的重點。
市場展望
◼ AI 需求持續擴張，關注財報季表現： AI 基礎建設需求未受利率環境影響，而是持續擴張，帶
動光通訊、CCL、ABF 載板 、記憶體、先進製程與封裝等關鍵供應鏈維持強勁成長，市場表
現由全面上漲轉向結構性分化。隨新一代 AI 伺服器放量、CSP 資本支出維持高檔，具供給優

勢、定價能力及訂單能見度延伸至 2027 年的龍頭廠商仍可望受惠。10 月中旬起陸續公布的
第三季財報，若結果持續表現亮眼，可望為市場再添動能，屆時具 AI 供應鏈題材的台股與日
股亦可望受惠。
◼ 債券佈局聚焦 5~10 年期美國公債及投等債：雖然貨幣政策風險 下降有助於穩定長期通膨預
期，但政府與企業發債供給增加所帶來的排擠效應，以及美國經濟成長展現韌性，仍可能使長
天期利率面臨上行壓力。因此，在當前利率環境下，5~10 年期債券較能兼顧票息收益與存續
期間風險管理。投資級債方面，彭博美國投資級公司債指數利差約 80-90 個基點，雖然利差
偏窄，但高基準利率環境仍提供具吸引力的絕對收益水準，因此現階段5~10 年期美國公債及
投資級債的風險報酬結構相對較佳。

ab 12 October 2026
Global Research
Genius Electronic Optical
Core resilience with CPO upside optionality
Raise PT to NT$1,250 on potential CPO contribution and intact core business
We reiterate Buy and raise our  price target to NT$1,250 from NT$700, rolling valuation
to 2028E and moving from P/B to a P/E-based SOTP that separately values the core lens
business and the emerging co-packaged optics (CPO) business. We believe Genius is
making progress sampling three precision components inside the fiber array unit (FAU)

targeting FAU integrators such as Coherent and FOCI.  We see potential for the company
to qualify the leading FAU module supplier into NVIDIA's CPO program. At the same
time, its core business is outperforming Largan, growing +1% YoY in Q326 vs. -11%
YoY for Largan as its share rises on periscope lens. Compared with the pressure on
Largan's margins from green electricity prices, we believe Genius should maintain its
margin, as its main production site is in Xiamen, whereas Largan's production in Taiwan

is affected by higher electricity prices.
Core intact: periscope share gains offset muted smartphone demand
September sales of NT$2.67bn (−2% MoM, +1% YoY) took Q326 to NT$7.82bn (+38%
QoQ, +1% YoY), in-line with VA consensus. Genius grew 10% YoY in 9M26 against 2%
at Largan, which we attribute to share gains in periscope lenses for premium iPhones,
offsetting legacy price cuts and lower VR lens shipments. We still keep 7% below VA on
4Q26E revenue (NT$7.34bn, −6% QoQ) reflecting muted iPhone sales and no content

in the iPhone 18 Pro's new variable-aperture lens.
CPO component strategy offers a different route to market from Largan
Unlike Largan's pursuit of complete FAU assembly, Genius is targeting high precision
optical components within the module, including: 1) high-precision prism to collimate
light from the PIC into the fibers, 2) V-groove/key slot to precisely hold the fibers in place,
and 3) MT ferrules (high density plastic connector bundling and aligning fibers). We

believe Genius' customer is one of the key module suppliers on NVIDIA's 2H27 program
targeting over 50% share. Our base case models a complete-FAU market of US$1.85bn
in 2028E and US$4.45bn in 2030E, with Genius base case for US$185m and US$668m
representing 10% and 15% revenue share of the complete-FAU revenue TAM.
Valuation: Maintain Buy with  PT lifted to NT$1,250
We fine-tune our 2026/2027 basic EPS estimates from NT$37.2/NT$41.5 to NT$36.7/NT

$44.0 and introduce 2028E EPS of NT$49.25. We move from P/B to P/E SOTP as the core
business now delivers steadier earnings and CPO warrants separate valuation. Our NT
$1,250 PT comprises NT$745 base business  (15x 2028E EPS of NT$49.25) and NT$505
for CPO (40x base case NT$12.72 EPS in 2028E). Catalysts include qualifications at
leading FAU makers and the board approved 7mn share private private placement with
potential strategic investor (albeit 6% dilution if issued). Our US$1.8bn CPO value

compares to US$7bn implied in our Largan NT$5,000 PT.

### Page 2

Genius Electronic Optical UBS Research
Thesis Map     UBS                    Research     THESIS MAP a guide to our thinking and what´s where in this report
Q: Could Genius' CPO products contribute meaningful revenue in a few years?
Yes, from H227E into 2028E. We see Genius' CPO strategy as feasible and lower-risk than Largan's.
Largan's goal is to supply the complete FAU; Genius is entering at the component level, supplying V-
grooves, MT ferrules and prisms to FAU makers. This lets Genius sell into multiple FAU designs and

integrators, limiting its downside if the market converges on a single winning architecture.
Management believes multi-sourcing is inevitable once volumes scale and has seen early indications
of potentially substantial demand from US optical players, including Corning and Coherent. Genius
has a partnership with FOCI and is pursuing other FAU integrators including we believe Coherent;
management suggested further updates could come in the near term. Some parts already ship in

small volumes, but we do not expect meaningful revenue until H227 or 2028.
Q: Will Genius benefit from further lens technology upgrades in the coming years?
Yes. We expect premium lens participation and product mix to support core revenue growth of 8.0%,
9.7% and 9.1% in 2026-28E. We believe Genius is gaining share from Largan in Apple's periscope
lens supply chain after initially lagging. Apple (70–80% of Genius' sales) sell-through units grew 9%

YoY YTD through August according to Counterpoint, and Genius' 9M26 revenue grew 10% YoY on
a similar trajectory. Largan grew only 2% in 9M26, with Q326 down 11% YoY. For the iPhone 18
series, Genius participates in every lens spec except the variable-aperture upgrade, where it stepped
back because of pricing pressure from Chinese competitors. Management says Genius has the
technical capability and could enter if future specs or design changes improve the opportunity.

Genius offers a combination of an established earnings base from smartphone lens and potential
exposure to the passive components required for optical interconnects. We remain constructive on
the lens business and see CPO as a potentially material addition from 2028. The component strategy
provides several routes to customers, but the valuation should reflect uncertainty over qualified
content, allocation and margins. Our Buy rating and NT$1,250 PT is based  on NT$745 base business

(15x 2028E EPS of NT$49.25) plus NT$505 for the CPO earnings (40x base case NT$12.72 EPS in
2028E). The CPO option for Genius is US$1.8bn vs. US$7bn implied in our Largan NT$5,000 PT.
Genius share gains are showing in reported sales, with YTD sales +10% YoY vs. +2% YoY for Largan.
The company is also participating in every spec except variable aperture.  For CPO, Genius delivered
samples of its prism, V-groove, and MT ferrule to FOCI at the end of May, with initial feedback broadly

on track and no major issues reported to date. Our checks indicate the company is also positioning to
supply one of the leading FAU module suppliers for NVIDIA's CPO program from H227.
At NT$1,060, the shares trade at 24.1x our 2027E core EPS and 21.5x our 2028E core EPS, factoring
some of the CPO opportunity in the multiple rather than in estimates relative to the 10 year average
17x P/E. Applying a 15x valuation to the smartphone lens business + 40x to the CPO option from our

scenario of NT$9.30 EPS in 2028E yields upside from the last close at NT$1,060 to a NT$1,250 share
price.

With CPO revenue contribution <5% in 2026 per management guidance, Genius' lens
business should remain its primary earnings anchor. With foldables shipping in 2H, Q3

shipment orders are broadly in line with historical patterns but should be watchful on
near-term demand trends as customers tend to adjust orders quickly in response to new
product sell-through evidenced by Largan's recent cuts. VR contribution has declined
from 5–10% in 2025 to <5% in 2026 on demand slowdown, partially offsetting
strength in phone lens while the mix shift would enhance Genius' overall margin profile.
On the other hand, AR is seeing traction supported by ongoing Ray-Ban shipments with

next-gen products in development.

### Page 4

Genius position in the CPO value chain
Genius is targeting the CPO market as a component supplier while Largan is advancing
from components into full-FAU assembly. Genius is sampling three components for CPO
with FAU makers, including: 1) high-precision prism to redirect light from the PIC into
the fibers, 2) V-groove/key slot to precisely hold the fibers in place, and 3) MT ferrules
(high density plastic connector bundling and aligning fibers). The companies overlap in

capability, with Largan also having PMLA and fiber arrays but not getting into some
components like MT ferrules and V-Grooves.
Genius's optical design, precision tooling, metrology and volume-manufacturing
experience offer it a good entry point for this market. Commercial differentiation will
depend on reproducible optical performance, dimensional tolerances, thermal stability
and customer yield.  It has announced collaboration with FOCI and  we believe has

potential to also work with one of NVIDIA's leading module suppliers for its H227
program.

Annual FAU TAM reaches US$1.85bn in 2028E and US$4.5bn in 2030E in

our base case
We update our FAU (fiber array unit) TAM factoring CPO/NPO penetration into
AI networking switches, NVIDIA multi-rack systems, scale-up on compute and switch
boards, and non-NVIDIA accelerators.  We assume one passive FAU assembly connecting
a fiber array to a photonic engine with one unit per optical engine.
We assume one FAU per optical engine and 32 per switch ASIC in our base case,
matching NVIDIA's 32x3.2T engines on its 102.4T Spectrum-X package. Broadcom's

102.4Tb/s Davisson uses 16 engines at 6.4Tb/s, so our bear case assumes an even mix of
these two designs (24 per ASIC). We note that higher switch bandwidth does not
automatically increase FAU count if engine bandwidth rises at the same rate. Our 2030E
assumption maintains the 32 FAUs per switch based on bandwidth rising to 204.8Tb/s

Our base case for the FAU market sums up 4 opportunities for CPO/NPO attach:
1. Scale-out and scale-across switches
We estimate annual switch ASIC shipments x CPO + NPO penetration x optical engines
per ASIC x FAUs per engine. In 2028E, 25% CPO/NPO penetration or 1.0m 102.4T
switch ASICs at 32 FAUs each produces 8.0m FAUs. In 2030E, 55% of 1.1mn

204.8T switch ASICs at 32 FAUs drives a market size of 19.4mn FAUs.

2. NVIDIA's multi-rack connections for Rubin Ultra and Feynman
NVIDIA at its 2026 GTC San Jose proposed a multi-rack option to network together 8
Oberon NVL72 racks for the Rubin Ultra generation and 8 Kyber NVL144 racks.

Feedback at the time showed  possible transceiver or NPO connections for Rubin Ultra
and CPO connections for Feynman.  Our 2028 base case uses 110,000 racks, 15% multi-
rack adoption and 75% CPO/NPO penetration, giving approximately 12,400 connected
racks.
For 2030E, our rack count falls to 35,000 racks as we assume Kyber would have 4x the
number of GPUs, equivalent to 140k Oberon equivalents. We assume the 35,000 racks
at 55% multi-rack adoption x 80% CPO/NPO attach gives 15,400 connected racks. We

assume about 216 and 576 inter-rack FAUs per rack, respectively, driving a market
opportunity of 2.7mn FAUs in 2028E and 8.9mn in 2030E.

Overall market opportunity: 12.3mn/35.6mn generates a US$1.85bn/US
$4.5bn TAM in 2028/2030E
We sum four pools: scale-out/scale-across switches, NVIDIA multi-rack pods, CPO native
NVIDIA GPUs and non-NVIDIA scale-up.  We add 10%/5% for FAUs lost at downstream
attach and test and for field spares, driving a market size of in our base case of 12.3mn
FAUs in 2028E and 35.6mn in 2030E. At US$150/US$125 per FAU, that is a US$1.85bn

market in 2028E and US$4.5bn in 2030E (bear/bull US$0.5bn/US$3.5bn in 2028E; US
$1.4bn/US$9.0bn in 2030E). The expansion to 2030E comes from wider optical
adoption and greater content in scale-up systems, despite an assumed FAU ASP decline
from US$150 to US$125.

Scenario analysis on Genius CPO opportunity

### Page 7

We assume in base case Genius captures 10% of the TAM in 2028E and 15% by 2030E.
The base case in 2028E is consistent with US$30 of prism, V-groove and ferrule content
in half of all FAUs or US$15 average content spread across the FAU market.  The
2030E bull case (25%) or US$37.50 would require near full content of some of the sub-
components or otherwise ability to integrate more of the solution value by that time.
Our bear case assumes only 1% share of the opportunity in 2028E and 5% of the

opportunity in 2030E, with EPS even by 2030E falling to NT$4 and yielding limited
valuation potential for the shares.

Reiterate Buy and raise PT to NT$1,250 valuing in CPO optionality

We adjust our valuation method from P/B to P/E SOTP to better reflect the company’s
core business and its opportunity within CPO industry while the core smartphone
business is demonstrating more stable earnings to use P/E vs. prior P/B. Our PT at NT
$1,250 is based on NT$745 base business  (15x 2028E EPS of NT$49.25) plus NT$505
for the CPO earnings (40x base case NT$12.72 EPS in 2028E) with catalysts from
upcoming qualifications into leading FAU makers and potential strategic private

placement with board approving 7mn share private placement (albeit 6% dilution if
issued). We note Genius CPO option valued at US$1.8bn vs. US$7bn implied in our
Largan valuation.
Our price target uses 2028 earnings for both businesses, reflecting the modeled start of
meaningful CPO contribution. We apply 15x core EPS of NT$49.25 and 40x CPO EPS of
NT$12.72 for a NT$1,250 price target. The 40x multiple assumes the optical business
can sustain strong growth after its initial ramp; qualification, concentration and margin

risk limit the confidence that can be placed on that earnings stream.
In the upside case, stronger premium lens participation and manufacturing execution lift
2028E core EPS to NT$54.32. Faster optical adoption expands the 2028E FAU market to
US$3.46bn; 15% effective revenue capture and a 31% pretax margin produce
approximately NT$36.88 CPO EPS. Applying 15x to core earnings and 42x to CPO
earnings gives approximately NT$2,365.
In the downside case, weaker lens demand and margin pressure lower core EPS to NT

$44.29. Slower optical adoption leaves a US$0.46bn FAU market, of which Genius
captures 1%, producing only NT$0.29 CPO EPS. Applying 12x and 38x gives
approximately NT$540. The downside is driven by both a weaker core business and a
much smaller optical contribution.

Upside/Downside Spectrum
Upside/Downside Spectrum
Genius (3406.TW) trading at 1,060 at closed Oct 8.
Source: UBS estimates
UPSIDE (NT$2,365): Lens upgrades accelerate and Genius gains share in higher-end
specs. 2027E/28E sales grow 11.8%/9.1% YoY (base 9.7%/9.1%), and GM expands to
35.6%/35.5% (base 34.4%/34.3%) on better yields and mix. 2028E core EPS of NT
$54.3 (+10% vs base) at 15x gives NT$815. Faster CPO adoption and stronger AI capex

lift the 2028E FAU TAM to US$3.5bn (19.8mn units at US$175); at 15% capture, CPO
sales reach US$519mn and CPO EPS NT$36.9, worth NT$1,550 at 42x.
BASE (NT$1,250):  Core NT$745 (15x NT$49.25) plus CPO NT$505 (40x NT$12.72), as
above.
DOWNSIDE (NT540): Smartphone demand weakens, legacy price erosion continues
and share gains stall. 2027E sales grow 7.4% YoY with GM of 33.4%, and 2028E core
EPS falls to NT$44.3 (−10% vs base), worth NT$530 at 12x. CPO adoption is slow

(10%/3% CPO/NPO in scale-out, no ASIC attach), leaving a US$0.46bn TAM; at 1%
capture, CPO is worth NT$11.

Company Description
Founded in 1989, Genius Electronic Optical (GSEO) is a leading global manufacturer of
camera lenses, providing full service optical solutions, including mobile lenses, automotive
lenses and AR/VR optics. Its production sites are in Taichung (one plant) and Xiamen (two

plants). The Taichung plant mainly manufactures VR lenses, while the Xiamen plants produce
compact lenses for consumer electronics. Optical lens products accounted for 73% of 2023
total revenue, and others include TOF lenses, flash lenses, VR lenses and LED lighting
products. Its major client is Apple and it supplies lenses for iPhones and iPads.
UBS EPS and Consensus EPS
The UBS EPS is an adjusted diluted EPS metric. It is calculated using the UBS analyst's

interpretation of earnings suitable for valuation purposes divided by the diluted number of
shares. This may differ to the way the consensus EPS metric has been calculated.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Our Genius price target is based on PE-based sum-of-the-parts model. Genius' growth is
highly leveraged to the overall handset industry and could be affected by volatility in handset
demand, as well as whether handset OEMs will continue upgrading their camera design.

Genius' margins could be affected by increasing competition and the rise of new
technologies, ASP pressure from customers and fluctuating material and labour costs.
Downside risks include competitors catching up in technology, yield rate issues for new
products and uncertain iPhone demand.
Our price target is based on SOTP valuation on the CPO opportunity and traditional lens
business. We think Largan's downside risks include: 1) ongoing pricing pressure from

smartphone OEM clients and increasingly intense competition among lens suppliers; and 2) a
slower-thanexpected smartphone demand recovery in China, and 3) CPO commercial success
below the market's elevated expectations. We believe the key upside risk is a faster-than-
expected CPO qualification process at Largan. In addition, if the company demonstrates
exceptionally efficient and highly accurate automation capabilities, it could support greater
market share gains within the industry than we currently anticipate.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

矽力*-KY (6415 TT) 
資料中心與車載為成長主軸

永豐觀點 
矽力杰 3Q26 營收優於預期，4Q26 展望正向，雖短期毛利率較為波動，不過隨資
料中心與車載訂單加持，以及第四代製程平滲透率提升， 應有助於中長期毛利率回
穩。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進：研究部預估矽力杰 2026 年稅後 EPS 11.95 元(原預估 11.59 元)，2027
年稅後 EPS 15.34 元。考量矽力杰資料中心與車載仍將持續帶動營運拓展，研究部
維持買進建議，目標價維持 644 元(42X 2027 年 EPS 評價)。

ESG 評析 
矽力*-KY 企業永續評鑑整體為 B 等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面
向平均得分分別為 B+等與 B 等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

矽力杰為電源管理晶片大廠：矽力杰成立於 02/2008，為電源管理 IC 設計公司，
產品線包括 DC/DC 轉換晶片、電池充電管理晶片、過流保護晶片、靜電防護裝置
與 LED 背光驅動晶片、能源監控管理晶片等。矽力杰 2Q26 產品終端應用主要區
分為四大領域，包括消費電子(如平板電腦、STB 與 TV)36%、工業應用(如 LED 照
明、伺服器與安防設備等)35%、資訊產品(如筆電)9%與網路通訊(如路由器、手機
等)6%、 車載14%。國際電源管理晶片設計同業與矽力杰產品類似者為 TI、Maxim、
ON Semi 與 ADI，台系類似的參與廠商則包括致新、茂達等。 
3Q26 營收優於預期 ，4Q26 展望正向 ：矽力杰總計 3Q26 營收 72.83 億元
(+21%QoQ，+53%YoY)，落於研究部原預估 3Q26 營收達成率 102%(研究部原
預估季增 18%)，優於預期，主要係由資料中心為最主要推升力道，再者車載因新
款晶片推出與市占率提升而增 、 消費電子即便市場需求較為疲弱， 但因公司新款晶
片推出，故反而仍能向上。目前展望 4Q26 亦維持正向看法，營收變化主要仍需視
後段封測產能的供給程度而定。研究部預估矽力杰 3Q26 毛利率 47.63%，稅後獲
利 11.32 億元(-28%QoQ，+66%YoY)，稅後 EPS 2.9 元，並預估 4Q26 營收 76.9
億元(+5.5%QoQ，+43%YoY)，毛利率 47.8%，稅後獲利 12.33 億元(+9%QoQ，
+53%YoY)，稅後 EPS 3.13 元。 
主要產能瓶頸在後段封測 ：目前矽力杰最主要的產能瓶頸在後段封測的支應，主要
是後段封測供給被 AI 應用排擠，目前除原有封測廠的產能擴充外，公司策略也持
續驗證新封測廠，以其紓解封測產能緊缺問題。至於晶圓產能，則矽力杰因具備第
四代 12 吋製程平台產能(第四代製程平台滲透率由 2025 年的個位數比重 ， 已將提
升至 4Q26 的 20%)，故反而晶圓較能充足的支應，明年第五代製程平台亦將加入
量產範圍。針對 2026 年毛利率走勢仍較有變數，係因影響毛利率因子正負交錯：
正向因子包括公司依據產品與客戶別而有不等的漲價措施 、 第四代製程平台挹注與
產品結構變化，負向因子則包括晶圓/封測成本上升、存貨依據庫齡政策而有損失
或回沖認列等影響。不過研究部認為隨 07/01 已開始進行漲價，理應能消弭部分
製造成本攀升的衝擊，遂預估 2H26 毛利率應能優於 2Q26 水準，中長期隨第四
代製程平台滲透率提升與產品結構變化， 理應有助於毛利率趨勢向上。 相較毛利率
是否獲得顯著提升，目前公司更關心在希冀產能能獲得更充足的支援， 以滿足客戶
的需求。 
資料中心與車載為後續成長主軸：資料中心、車載與工業將成為推升後續成長的主
軸：(1) 資料中心(包括主板/企業級 SSD/光模組)目前營收比重 15%~16%，2027
年 目 前 維 持 預 期>20% 比 重 ， 公 司資 料 中 心 三 大 項 目 比 重 依 序 為光 通 訊
>SSD>MB，其中光通訊與 SSD 個別均為 mid-to-high single digit，MB 比重較
低。公司過往以 400G 以下為主，今年 400G/800G 均已導入，隨市占率逐步提
升，又未來若主板核心電源出貨，更將進一步推動資料中心業務規模擴大。公司亦
表示 PSU 相關產品出貨亦隨伺服器需求而增，公司 PSU 產品包括 AC-DC 
controller/ESD/Boost/ Buck/Temp sensor等，目前針對 VPD 進展還沒那麼快。
(2) 車載營收比重可望由去年的 13%， 攀升至今年的15%~20%， 研究部認為車載
動力來自於公司車載晶片供應品項增加， 每台車的可供應的內含價值擴大， 並且客
戶群攀升，除中國品牌上量外，也進一步拓展海外市場。(3) 儲能與光伏需求，以
及充電樁應用，亦能拓展工業領域的需求。 
維持買進： 研究部預估矽力杰 2026 年營收 258.35 億元(+37%YoY)，毛利率
47.65%，稅後獲利 47.05 億元(+90%YoY)，稅後 EPS 11.95 元(原預估 11.59 元)，
並預估 2027 年營收 341.9 億元(+32%YoY)，毛利率 48.44%，稅後獲利 60.39 億
元(+28%YoY)，稅後 EPS 15.34 元。考量矽力杰資料中心與車載仍將持續帶動營

營運現況與分析

목표주가 630,000원 (U)  
 직전 목표주가 530,000원  
 현재주가 (9/22) 276,500원  
 상승여력 128%

시가총액 16,164,960억원  
 총발행주식수 6,648,649,811주

 절대 (1.8) (21.8) 231.1   
 상대 (3.2) 1.6  63.7   
 절대 (달러환산) 0.3  (11.5) 239.5

3Q26 전사 예상 영업이익 100조원 
DS부문 예상 영업이익은 100.0조원(OPM 67.4%, YoY +1538.5%, QoQ +14.5%) 으로, 
달러환율 1,380원기준, 메모리와 비메모리 사업부문 예상 영업손익은 각각 102.0조원,    
-2.0조원으로 추정한다. 1) 메모리반도체 가격 상승세가 이어지는 가운데, HBM4 판매 
본격화에 따른 영향을 고려하면 DRAM/NAND 분기 평균 가격 상승폭은 각각 +18.0%, 
+16.0%으로 추정하고, 2)비메모리반도체 사업 적자는 지속될 전망이기 때문이다. 다만 
특별 성과급 지급에 따른 충당금 영향을 제외하고 선단공정 중심의 파운드리 가동률을 
고려하면 적자폭은 유의미하게 감소한 것으로 파악된다는 점이 긍정적이다.   
SDC부문 예상 영업이익은 1.1조원(OPM 13.0%, YoY -9.6%, QoQ +54.9%)으로, 1)국내 
주력 고객사향 폴더블 스마트폰 판매 호조가 사업부의 외형 성장에 긍정적으로 작용했을 
것으로 추정됨에도 불구하고, 2) 8.6세대 IT OLED 패널 양산 개시에 따른 감가상각비(약 
0.3조원 수준)와 북미 고객 중심의 판가 인하 압박 영향으로 전년동기대비 수익성은 약 
2%pt 훼손이 불가피할 전망이다. DX부문 예상 영업손익은 -1.1조원(YoY 적자전환, QoQ 
적자지속)으로, 메모리반도체를 포함한 IT 부품 가격 부담이 전분기대비 확대되고 있기 
때문이다. MX와 VD/DA사업부 예상 손익은 각각 -0.9조원, -0.2조원으로 추정한다.

투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 63만원으로 상향 조정  
메모리반도체의 가격 상승폭이 다소 둔화되겠지만, 2027~2028년에도 타이트한 수급 
환경이 이어질 전망이라는 점을 주목한다. 1)시장은 신규 증설과 HBM 관련 노이즈로 인한 
2028년 피크아웃 가능성을 우려하고 있으나, HBM 의 범용 DRAM 생산능력 잠식과 AI 
추론 확산에 따른 고용량 메모리 수요를 감안하면 공급 제약의 해소 시점은 예상보다 
늦어질 가능성이 높기 때문이다. 뿐만 아니라, 2)메모리 업사이클이 장기화될 경우 동사 
FCF는 과거대비 구조적으로 확대되어 보다 친화적인 주주환원 정책(2027~2029년, 
2027년 1월 예정)을 제시할 가능성도 배제할 수 없기 때문이다. 동사 2027년 연간 실적 
추정치 상향 조정에 따라 목표주가를 63만원(2027년 BPS * Target PBR 3.5배 적용)으로 
상향 조정한다. 피크아웃 우려에 따른 주가 조정은 중장기 관점에서 매수 기회다.

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 파업 영향에도 견조한 실적, 4분기 성장 확대삼성바이오로직스(207940) 파업 영향에도 견조한 실적, 4분기_22fa3b02.pdf

제약업종

◼ 3Q26 파업 영향에도 컨센서스 상회 
◼ 4Q26 지연 물량 인식 및 신규 공장 기여로 실적 개선 전망 
◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
목표주가는 DCF 방식으로 산출(WACC 8.1%, 영구성장률 3.5% 적용). 기
존 공장의 높은 가동률을 기반으로 5공장 매출 기여 확대, 미국 생산거점 
확보, 신규 모달리티 진출을 통한 중장기 성장 지속 전망.

3Q26 Review: 파업 영향에도 컨센서스 상회  
3Q26 매출액 1조 4,903억원(+18.5% yoy, +12.8% qoq), 영업이익 6,401
억원(+1.1% yoy, +9.1% qoq), 영업이익률 42.9%(-7.5%p yoy)를 기록하며 
당사 추정치 및 시장 컨센서스 상회. 5월 1차 파업에 따른 매출 차질(약 
1,500억원) 에도 불구하고, 기존 1~4공장 Full 가동이 지속되며 견조한 실
적 달성. 5공장 PPQ 배치 생산 확대와 및 미국 록빌 공장 매출 인식도 외
형 성장에 기여. 다만, 신규 생산시설 초기 가동비와 제품 믹스 영향으로 
영업이익률은 전년 동기 대비 하락.

4Q26 매출액 1조 5,764억원(+22.6% yoy), 영업이익 7,075억원
(+34.0% yoy) 전망  
4Q26에는 2분기 파업으로 지연된 생산물량의 순차적인 매출 인식과 미국 
록빌 공장 매출 기여, 5공장 PPQ 배치 생산 확대로 실적 개선 예상, 동사
는 신규 생산시설의 매출 가시성 확보를 바탕으로 2026년 매출 성장률 
20%를 무난히 달성할 것으로 전망(연초 가이던스 15~20%).

신규 수주 회복과 포트폴리오 확장에 주목 
미국 의약품 관세정책 불확실성 완화로 지연됐던 수주 협상이 재개되며 신
규 수주 환경 개선 중. 3Q26 약 3억달러의 신규 수주를 확보했으며, 대형 
계약 협상도 구체화되고 있어 2027년 수주 회복 기대. 이는 5공장 가동률 
확대와 중장기 성장 가시성을 높이는 요인. 6공장은 ‘26년말~’27년초 착
공을 목표로 기존 증설 일정 유지. 
폴리펩타이드 그룹 인수는 1차 공개매수 응모율 87.88%를 기록하며 연내 
인수 완료 추진 중. 인수 완료 시 펩타이드 CDMO로 사업영역이 확대되며 
글로벌 생산거점 및 고객 기반 다변화 기대.

## Document 230: AI崩了、法国爆了，高盛推荐“对冲策略”：关注日元！

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI崩了、法国爆了，高盛推荐“对冲策略”：关注日元！.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

⾼盛 FICC 团队将 AI 估值崩塌、法国债券危机、⽇元低估三条主线编织成同⼀个宏观故事：
美国例外论正在⽡裂，资本正在回头。其核⼼结论是 —— ⽇元被低估 40% ，⽽ 2 年期隐含波
动率仅 9.9 ，远低于 20 年均值，这意味着以近历史最低的价格，为估值偏离最⼤的货币买⼊
保护。当 AI 下跌、美债收益率⾛低、美元兑⽇元⾛软的相关性链条已成型，持有⽇元，就
是当前最廉价的分散化⼯具。
摘要：
⾼盛 FICC 团队将三条看似⽆关的市场主线 ——AI 估值重挫、法国债券危机与⽇元被低估 —— 串
联成同⼀个故事，并向机构客⼾推荐以⽇元多头作为当前最具性价⽐的对冲⼯具。
⾼盛 FICC 交易员 Jonathon Chryssafis 在最新发布的报告中指出， AI 巨头财报令市场失望，正在
重燃外界对这⼀科技趋势持续性的担忧。特斯拉削减资本开⽀、 Meta 的 AI ⽀出推动其信⽤违约
互换（ CDS ）升⾄历史⾼位、 Lam Research 业绩指引令⼈失望，三重利空叠加，令对冲基⾦加

速撤离科技板块。
与此同时，法国国债与德国国债的利差已扩⾄欧元诞⽣以来最宽⽔平，法国 10 年期国债收益率
突破 5% ，超过欧债危机时期的绝对⽔平。⾼盛认为，上述⻛险最终都指向同⼀个⽅向：美元资
产吸引⼒下降、资本回流⽇本，进⽽推动⽇元升值。⾼盛的⻓期公允价值模型显⽰，⽇元相对
美元被低估约 40% ，是其追踪货币中估值最为偏低的⼀个。
AI 信贷裂缝：数据中⼼债券成为⻛暴眼
Chryssafis 在报告中强调， AI 估值重挫的真正震中并⾮股票市场，⽽是信⽤市场 —— 尤其是低评
级借款⼈的债券。
数据显⽰， 2026 年迄今（截⾄ 10 ⽉ 13 ⽇），美元项⽬融资债券发⾏规模已达约 1050 亿美元，⼏
乎全部流向数据中⼼及芯⽚ / 算⼒融资项⽬。这⼀规模约为 AI 时代之前历史最⾼纪录（ 2020 年约
250 亿美元）的四倍，也是 2025 年（已是当时历史纪录）的近三倍。
⾼盛信⽤策略团队对美元投资级科技债维持低配评级，并在其《 AI 、信⽤与更⾼资本成本》报告
中指出，在利率 " 更⾼更久 " 及潜在能源成本压⼒的背景下，市场正在重新评估 AI 建设的经济逻

辑。在⾼收益债领域，⾼盛维持中性，但预计 AI 相关发⾏⼈之间的分化将持续扩⼤， " 尤其是项
⽬融资债券领域 " 。 Bernstein ⽬前估计 CoreWeave 存在 760 亿美元的融资缺⼝，所谓 " ⾼度分
化 " 或许意味着部分债券得到偿付、部分则不然。
法国债市 " ⻜出轨道 " ：欧元承压
欧洲⽅⾯，法国债券危机持续升温。法德 10 年期利差已扩⾄ 173 个基点，不仅超过 2011 年欧债
危机时期的⽔平，更创下欧元诞⽣以来的历史最宽纪录。法国 10 年期国债收益率达到 5.08% ，
⽽意⼤利国债收益率⽬前已较法国低出约 50 个基点 —— 这⼀倒置在 2011 年危机期间⼏乎⽆法想
象。
⾼盛策略师认为，能源价格下⾏是压缩利差的最清晰路径，原因在于：其⼀，可改善增⻓与财
政⾚字的⻛险偏斜；其⼆，有助于市场回归 ECB 有限加息的基准预期，从⽽带来⼀定的核⼼利
率缓解；其三，可降低利率波动率，提升投资者重返套利交易的意愿。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
5 收藏 分享到 :
写评论
Chryssafis 表⽰，若上述能源价格缓解未能实现， " 预计将看到更宽的利差和欧元汇率承受更⼤
压⼒ " 。⾼盛交易台近期已观察到客⼾对欧元兑美元下⾏期权的需求上升，隐含波动率在⻓期历
史回溯中仍处于相对低位。从估值⻆度看，欧元兑美元 5 年期实际利率差已收窄⾄ -136 个基点，
与 2025 年初欧元交投于 1.03 附近时的⽔平相当，⽽当前欧元兑美元仍在 1.12 附近，两者之间的
缺⼝意味着欧元存在进⼀步下⾏空间。
⽇元：被低估 40% 的最廉价对冲⼯具
报告中最引⼈关注的部分，是⾼盛将 AI 交易与⽇元⾛势直接挂钩。
⾼盛的 GSDEER ⻓期公允价值模型显⽰，⽇元相对美元被低估约 40% ，是该⾏追踪货币名单中
估值偏离最⼤的货币。其背后逻辑在于：⽇本数⼗年来将过剩储蓄⼤规模配置于美国⾦融资

产，⼀旦 AI 引发的 " 美国例外论 " 叙事动摇、美⽇利差收窄，资本回流⽇本的趋势将随之加速，表
现为美元抛售、⽇元买⼊。
⾼盛交易台指出，三重结构性因素已天然指向⽇元⾛强：⽇本官员持续表达对强势⽇元的⽀
持；⽇本⾸相⾼市早苗（ Takaichi ）积极推动国内资⾦加⼤对本⼟⾦融资产的配置；⽇本央⾏维
持渐进加息路径，⽽⽇本最⼤劳⼯团体⽬前正寻求在 2027 年春季薪资谈判中设定 6% 的⽬标中值
（据路透社），这为⽇本央⾏继续加息提供了充分理由。
更重要的是，对冲成本极低。 2 年期美元兑⽇元平值期权隐含波动率⽬前为 9.9 ，远低于 20 年均
值 13.8 ，接近 20 年低点 8.4 。⾼盛认为，以接近历史低位的价格为其追踪名单中估值最低的货币
购买保护，性价⽐突出。
相关性翻转： AI 下跌即⽇元上涨
⾃ 10 ⽉ 14 ⽇（⾼盛 AI 领先股票篮⼦单⽇下跌 4.7% ）以来，该篮⼦与标普 500 的相关性从 0.73 跳
升⾄ 0.91 ；与 10 年期美债收益率变动的相关性从 -0.13 翻转为 +0.19 ；与美元兑⽇元的相关性则

从 -0.34 翻转为 +0.46 。
这意味着，当前市场的逻辑链条已清晰成型：AI 下跌 → 标普下跌 → 美债收益率下⾏ → ⽇元升
值。⾼盛交易台表⽰，将继续看好 " 股票下⾏ / 收益率下⾏ " 双重⼆元期权作为增⻓冲击对冲⼯
具，并特别指出近期已实现相关性已回归 "AI 下跌、 10 年期收益率⾛低、美元兑⽇元⾛软 " 的模
式。
唯⼀可能打破上述逻辑的情景，是⾼盛⾃⾝提出的布伦特原油冲⾄ 140 美元以上的尾部⻛险 ——
届时收益率将随油价上⾏，股票同步下跌，双重对冲策略将失效。但在此之前，随着标普 500 与
单⼀ AI 篮⼦的相关性已升⾄ 0.91 ， " 分散化 " 在当下有了新的含义：持有⽇元。
请发表您的评论

## Document 261: Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

By Advanced Economies Team
Advanced Economies:
Resilience to shock after shock
October 2026

### Page 2

• The global economy is in the midst of a growth cycle. As a result, GDP growth is more resilient to shocks than initially
anticipated.
• The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise in oil prices, whose negative impact on growth is offset
by a series of reflationary forces.
• The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial intelligence (AI).
• The world’s major economies are currently engaged in a broader push to expand their capital goods investment.

• While European and US households are experiencing more or less the same energy price shock, inflation and household
consumption remain higher in the US.
• Higher and above-target inflation calls for monetary tightening.
• Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks examined in this publication (the US, the UK, the
Eurozone). Inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026 or Q1 2027, but there is a significant risk that energy inflation will

spread further to other components of inflation in the future.
• Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength (in the US).
• Monetary policies also have to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal policy (particularly in Japan).
• The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: the ensuing rise in energy prices contributes to the recent increase in
long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the start of the year) to levels close to those of 2002.

• The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period of growth), reflecting different fiscal trajectories. In the
US, the UK, France and Germany the public debt-to-GDP ratio is increasing, while it has stabilized in Italy and decreased in
Spain and Japan.
• The rise in long-term interest rates will weigh on public finances. The fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it will be in 2030.
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with GDP growth.

For more information, please refer to our publication:
EcoPerspectives – Advanced Economies | 3rd quarter of 2026, as of September 24, 2026– Economic Research– BNP Paribas
Advanced economies: Resilience to shock after shock

## Document 272: CPO概念大跌，“易中天”集体回应！

Source PDF: all report pdf/CPO概念大跌，“易中天”集体回应！.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

10 ⽉ 22 ⽇， “ 易中天 ” 股价同步⼤跌，新易盛跌超 12% ，中际旭创、天孚通信跌超 8% 。⾯对
市场关注，三家公司均回应称⽣产经营、订单交付正常。其中，中际旭创表⽰供应链稳
定、产能利⽤率充⾜；天孚通信称 CPO/NPO 提产节奏未⻅明显变化，相关供应改善有望在
三、四季度逐步释放业绩影响。
摘要：
10 ⽉ 22 ⽇，光模块⻰头 " 易中天 " 组合（新易盛、中际旭创、天孚通信）同步重挫：新易盛跌
12.28% ，较年内⾼点回撤超 56% ；中际旭创跌 8.76% ，较年内⾼点回撤超 56% ，全天成交额超
173 亿元、位居 A 股第⼀；天孚通信跌 8.36% 。华盛昌、崇达技术跌停，剑桥科技、联讯仪器、
源杰科技等跌幅靠前， CPO 、铜缆⾼速连接、 PCB 、元件、先进封装等概念整体低迷。
据证券时报，针对市场关注的公司经营情况，源杰科技表⽰，公司⽬前⽣产经营活动、订单交
付、产能扩建等⼯作正常开展。新易盛表⽰，公司⽬前⽣产经营活动正常有序，订单交付⼯作
正常开展。此外，东⼭精密也表⽰，⽬前公司⽣产经营⼀切正常，产品交付也在有序推进中。

源杰科技：订单交付正常  市场需求旺盛
据证券时报，针对市场关注的公司经营情况，公司表⽰⽬前⽣产经营活动、订单交付、产能扩
建等⼯作正常开展。公司相关公开信息显⽰，⾯向数据中⼼领域的 CW 激光器是今年交付的主⼒
产品，市场需求旺盛。
公司产能扩充正按业务需求推进，并于近期披露了光电通讯半导体芯⽚和器件研发⽣产基地⼆
期项⽬进展，项⽬投资总额约 12.51 亿元。该项⽬的⼟建⼯程、部分洁净室及相关配套设施已完
成建设，⾸批⽣产设备预计近期开始进⼚，后续将进⾏设备调试，预计 2026 年底⾄ 2027 年初开
始形成产能。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

天孚通信：⽬前发布的相关进出⼝政策未对光器件产品进⾏约束  不涉及对
公司业务产⽣影响
天孚通信发布投资者关系活动记录表公告，公司为 CPO 技术路线配套的 FAU 、 ELS 等产品在性
能、良率及交付保障等⽅⾯已具备较好基础，会根据客⼾需求持续提产。 NPO 相关配套产品⽬
前处于样品阶段。 CPO/NPO 的提产节奏近期未出现明显变化。
有源光器件关键瓶颈物料供给情况较上半年有所改善，从物料到货到成品出货交付需要⼀定周
期，预计相关供应改善对公司经营业绩的积极影响将在第三季度、第四季度逐步释放。
⽆源器件需求还在持续快速增加，公司在积极推动产能扩充以提升整体产出。公司明年⽆源器
件扩产将同时覆盖可插拔光模块和 CPO/NPO ⽅向，⼆者均有较强的需求⽀撑，公司将统筹资源
配置，在保障现有业务增⻓的同时，积极⽀持新技术⽅向的导⼊与放量。
⽬前发布的相关进出⼝政策未对光器件产品进⾏约束，不涉及对公司业务产⽣影响。公司在泰
国建有量产基地，能满⾜客⼾全球多基地扩产的需要。
新易盛：订单交付有序开展

光通信板块 22 ⽇整体回调，新易盛股价也出现⼤跌。针对市场关注的公司经营情况，公司表⽰
⽬前⽣产经营活动正常有序，订单交付⼯作正常开展。
公司相关公开信息亦显⽰， 800G 和 1.6T 是今年交付的主⼒产品，订单交付及产能扩充正按业务
需求推进。

## Document 298: Five ways the UK’s Autumn Budget could shock us

Source PDF: all report pdf/Five ways the UK’s Autumn Budget could shock us.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From better ﬁscal headroom to surprise income tax hikes, this
is how UK Chancellor John Healey's ﬁrst budget could surprise
investors
James Smith
UK Chancellor John Healey
In a nutshell: What we expect
Higher debt interest costs look set to drive the level of 'ﬁscal
headroom' from £24bn in March to £10bn. There's a growing
sense that Chancellor John Healey would like to get this up
to £15bn or higher. The government is set to modestly
increase spending on the cost of living, though the total

intervention is unlikely to meaningfully top £5bn and it's
likely to be mostly billed as temporary to avoid counting
against the main ﬁscal rule. We expect an increase in capital
spending of roughly £10bn/year, bearing in mind this is
exempt from the ﬁscal rule dictating that the current budget
must be in surplus after ﬁve years. To rebuild headroom

### Page 2

against that rule, we expect £5-6bn of tax hikes, likely via a
higher Bank Surcharge and possibly broadening the National
Insurance tax base.
In short, we don't expect the budget to deliver a material
stimulus (or add materially to the already-planned ﬁscal
consolidation). The impact on the Bank of England, which is
poised to hike rates in November, is likely to be fairly
minimal.
But all chancellors like to keep a rabbit in their hat – and what
follows are ﬁve ways it could surprise us.

1. Fiscal headroom goes up – not down
What if the UK’s ﬁscal hole isn’t nearly as bad as we think?
The widespread expectation going into the UK’s Autumn Budget on
28 October is that the Chancellor is going to have to face up to
higher debt interest costs with some modest tax hikes.
Bank of England rate expectations are almost a percentage point
higher than they were at the time of the Oﬃce for Budget
Responsibility’s March assessment of Britain’s public ﬁnances. 10-

year bond yields are some 70 basis points higher, which, taken
together, will push up debt interest spending by £11bn/year by
2029/30.
That points to a sizeable reduction in the amount of money the
government has left under its main ﬁscal rule, which requires the
current budget (day-to-day spending, including debt interest less
revenue) to be in surplus within three years. Back in March, that
surplus, or ﬁscal headroom, was projected at £24bn in 2029/30.

Debt interest costs are rising

### Page 3

*Based on the average rate in the 10 days up to 25 September, which
likely represents the OBR's observation window for this budget
Source: Macrobond, ING
We think that headroom will have dropped to around £10bn before
any corrective action. And the vibe from the Treasury suggests the
Chancellor would prefer it to be above £15bn, to give more
breathing space in case of future adverse forecast revisions. That
would point to some limited tax hikes.
But what if that’s all wrong? The truth is, nobody really knows how

the OBR will change its assessment of the UK economy. And as we
saw last year, the Treasury is often inclined to talk up the doom and
gloom to open up a positive surprise on the day. 12 months ago,
the hit to headroom was considerably smaller than had been
widely expected.
This time, any positive surprise is most likely to come from wage
growth. Will the rise in energy prices spill into higher pay, just as it
did in 2022? We think it won’t, not least because the jobs market is

much weaker, an assessment we suspect the OBR will share.
But even relatively small upgrades to wage growth can make a big
diﬀerence to the government’s fortunes. Since 2010, the OBR has
revised the level of earnings across its ﬁve-year forecast by an
average of 1.3 percentage points in absolute terms. If it did that
now, say by lifting wage growth over the next two years by 0.6ppt
apiece, it would add £10bn to headroom, almost perfectly
oﬀsetting the hit from higher interest costs.

### Page 4

There are also reasons to think the impact of lower migration
projections, which some reports suggest could wipe another £7bn
oﬀ headroom via lower tax revenue forecasts, could be much less
than feared. The starting point for employment looks a little higher
than it did back in March. The rise in global equities this year should
also be helpful for capital gains tax projections.
Why does this all matter? Because the higher the headroom, the
less ‘need’ there is to hike taxes, and if it is signiﬁcantly higher than

expected, the more room there is to increase spending under the
ﬁscal rules.
The simple message is this: don’t judge the Autumn Budget by the
level of headroom left over.
2. Spending splurge
The problem with ‘headroom’ is not only that it’s highly sensitive to
the OBR’s economic outlook, but that the forward-looking nature of
the ﬁscal rules opens the door to a lot of creative accounting.
The impact of higher inﬂation is a case in point. This helps the

Treasury through higher receipts – both VAT and income tax. Yes, it
also pushes up the cost of indexed beneﬁts. But importantly,
departmental budgets don’t automatically increase to oﬀset the
impact of higher prices.
That opens up a classic trick from the Treasury, which is to leave
spending plans virtually unchanged in nominal terms, setting up
substantially smaller real-terms budget increases across the
medium term.
Even before the Iran War, government plans pencilled in real-terms

cuts to per capita departmental spending in FY2029. Given some
functions, like health, are ‘protected’, that implies much deeper cuts
to smaller departments.
Suﬃce it to say, that is unrealistic. Higher inﬂation will push up the
wage bill across government. It would cost £12bn to simply
maintain those March departmental spending plans in real terms.
That’s before considering the cost of ending that austerity pencilled

### Page 5

in for later this decade, or how the government will fund its defence
or social care ambitions.
A top-up to government departments is inevitable – the question is
when. It looks like the Treasury wants to kick the can on these tricky
budget decisions until next autumn, when it plans to release its
next Spending Review, a process of allocating money across
departments. No doubt the hope is that debt interest costs will
have fallen and the economic outlook brightened, such that there's

more space under the ﬁscal rules to make bigger spending
commitments.
That points to a very modest package of spending measures this
month, mainly targeting the cost of living. On top of the already-
announced cut to VAT on energy, we’d imagine the Treasury will
replicate last year’s budget and lower household bills by funding a
greater share of ‘policy costs’ from general taxation. This has the
added beneﬁt of lowering headline inﬂation at a particularly

sensitive moment for the Bank of England. Crucially though, the
government is likely to bill these moves as temporary, so they don’t
count under that main ﬁscal rule.
But if the economic picture really is better than feared, don’t rule
out the government going further. A decision to fully fund the UK’s
3% defence spending commitment would be a major wildcard,
though this would likely come at the expense of capital plans in
other areas of government.

Ultimately, the key thing to watch is the OBR’s assessment of how
all these policy changes aﬀect GDP growth and borrowing over the
next year. If there’s a material stimulus eﬀect, it could add to
market bets on UK rate hikes.
3. A manifesto break on income tax
Tax hikes are likely at the Autumn Budget, but should be very small.
We’re probably looking at a package of circa £5bn (0.2% of GDP).
Part of the problem is that the list of viable options looks perilously

small if Labour is to avoid breaking its manifesto pledge not to raise

the major taxes, which together account for around 70% of total
revenue.
Our best guess is that the budget will include a hike in the Bank
Surcharge (raising £2bn if increased from 3% to 8%), together with
a broadening of the tax base for National Insurance to landlords
and partners in LLPs (together yielding around £2bn/year). Other
options, like ending a loophole on stamp duty for commercial
property or lowering the VAT threshold for businesses, have also

been on the table.
The problem with many of these measures is that they require
massive changes to stand any chance of raising material sums,
risking big behavioural reactions while oﬀering uncertain revenue
potential. There’s a reason why former chancellor Rachel Reeves
shied away from many of the measures we outline in the chart
below.
How the Chancellor could raise taxes

Is there scope for a surprise? We think there are two main ways on
tax – one good, one bad.
The good? A surprise announcement of a so-called ‘tax switch’, a
novel proposal from Resolution Foundation which would see the
government simultaneously raise basic income tax and cut

employee National Insurance by 2ppt. The impact on workers
would be neutral, but because income tax has a broader base
(including pensions), this would raise £6bn/year.
Though not a substantial sum, it would signal to investors that
Prime Minister Andy Burnham isn’t afraid to go near the thorny
issue of raising taxes. But in light of recent pension changes,

### Page 8

including to the infamous ‘triple lock’, this feels like too much of a
political gamble at this stage.
The negative surprise would be if the Treasury decided to raise the
basic tax allowance. Burnham ﬂirted with this on his ﬁrst day in
oﬃce; it would not only be expensive (potentially £10bn), but it
would also signal that the UK’s commitment to the threshold freeze
– which is doing the lion's share of the planned ﬁscal consolidation
– is waning.
A big rise in the National Living Wage

The minimum wage increase in April 2027, which will be
announced alongside the budget, is perhaps the most important
variable for the Bank of England.
This is based on a recommendation from the Low Pay Commission,
which earlier this year provisionally estimated the increase at 3.7%.
That’s a smaller increase than over the past two years and,
crucially, is not a million miles above the level the Bank considers
consistent with a 2% medium-term inﬂation target (3.25%).

But the LPC has some ﬂexibility in its recommendation, and one
criterion is the cost of living. Higher inﬂation means we could see a
ﬁnal proposal between 4% and 4.5%. Anything above that would,
we suspect, be problematic for the Bank of England and would
bolster the case for higher rates.
The government is no doubt well aware of this. And there appears
to be growing recognition among Labour ﬁgures, including on the
left of the party, that the big minimum wage increases, coupled

with previous payroll tax increases, have had a big impact on
business – particularly in consumer services.
But if the LPC were to recommend minimum wage growth of close
to 5%, could the government resist it? In theory, yes. But politically,
we suspect not.
A surge in bond issuance
We already know that bond issuance is set to increase again in
2027, though for reasons unrelated to the UK’s ﬁscal trajectory.

The chart below shows that in April, the Debt Management Oﬃce

number in FY2025. We suspect that when we get the updated DMO
remit following the budget, next year’s issuance could be closer to
£325bn – though a greater chunk of that will likely get funded using
bills, meaning the actual pick-up in gilt sales relative to those
previous plans is likely to be more modest.

Still, the point is that, beneath these numbers, public sector net
borrowing is still likely to be projected to keep falling through the
rest of the decade. The major risk – related to some of the things

discussed earlier, like larger near-term spending or surprise
increases in tax allowances – would jeopardise this fundamental
improvement in bond issuance in the medium term. That's
something investors wouldn’t take kindly to.

高 。 繼上週歐洲 央 行 將 存 款 利 率 調 升 至
2.50%後，日本銀行 也加速升 息，基準利率
來到 1.25% 、 態 度 轉 鷹， 全球債 市先跌後
漲，因部分投資人預期債 市已提前反映升息
風險，高殖利率開始吸引資金進場鎖利。
► 聯準會緊縮後年底前仍有升息一碼預期 ，美
元指數重回百元之上 ，非美貨幣 貶值。加密
貨幣監管的重要法案《 CLARITY法案》，在
美國參議院終止辯論程序 性表決中 ，法案以
49票對50票未獲通過 ，無法進入最終表決程
序，虛擬資產聞訊下 挫。沙烏地阿拉伯關閉
東西輸油管線，佔全球原油供應 量約4-5%，
加深能源供需失衡 疑慮，沙國透過阿曼港口

出口、緩解供給 ，油價先漲後跌 ，西德州油
價保持每桶百元高價 。聯準會會後 ，美債利
率利空出盡下滑，激勵金、銅價反彈。

► 聯準會9月升息1碼至3.75%-4.00%，最新點
陣圖顯示2026年仍有再升息一碼空間 ，2027
年傾向維持利率不變 ，2028年後才逐步回到
降息趨勢 。主席華許重申抑制通膨目標 ，升
息立場官員數明顯較前次會議增加 ，凸顯聯
準會鷹派政策態度 ，惟利率路徑較市場原先
預期的連續緊縮和緩，市場偏利多解讀。
► 聯準會 本次亦上修 2026-2027年GDP成長預
估，下修失業率預測 ，反映美國 經濟與勞動
市場尚未明顯轉弱；另一方面 ，能源價格上
升與成本轉嫁使整體及核心通膨預估再度上

修；景氣具韌性 、通膨回落速度偏慢 ，使聯
準會仍有條件維持較緊縮的政策立場 。在高
利率環 境可 能延 續 下，股 票評價 仍面 臨壓
力，配置上可相對偏向獲利與現金流穩定 、
防禦性較高的產業；債券則以中短天期 、高
信評企業債為主要方向 ，把握目前較高的收
益率。長天期公債仍受通膨 、財政紀律及期
限溢酬上升影響 ，宜待通膨與景氣出現更明
確降溫訊號後，再提高配置。

► 日本8月CPI年增1.9%，油價上漲帶來的外溢
影響，逐漸反應在消費成本上。7月勞工平均
薪資較去年同期成長 4.7%，創1997年1月以
來最大增幅；薪資增速追上通膨 ，帶動實質
薪資增長 。此外 ，第二季 GDP季增年率達
1.4%優於預期，8月製造業PMI因AI及半導體
需求暢旺上升，7月出口年增率高達23.2%，
創2022年10月來最快增速 。整體通膨升溫加
上景氣復甦，為日銀升息提供底氣。
► 日本央行會後 聲明稿表示：金融環境實質利
率偏低 ，將視情況調整貨幣政策 ，並強調升
息後金融環境仍維持寬鬆 ，未如市場預期的
鷹派，使日股持續上漲 、日圓回貶 。考量日
銀呵護 寬鬆金融環境 ，侷限日圓續強力 道，
但在美 國施 壓與 日 銀未來 仍可能 升息 背景
下，日圓難回到過去極弱 狀態。相較過去 ，
現在升值的日圓對日本出口 、科技股壓力可
控，日本10年公債殖利率來到3%，利率上升
有利 金融 股 ，投 資 短期或 以金融 、內 需為

主，科技、半導體股逢回可分批布局。
經濟與通膨升溫，日銀加快緊縮步調

投資焦點低波價值防禦
► 美、歐、日主要央行連袂升息 ，加上美伊衝
突惡化 ，國際油價高漲 ，通膨疑慮與成熟國
家債務壓力 ，使全球多數國家公債殖利率衝
高，市場擔憂高利率與高油價可能拖累經濟
成長，全球股債匯市承壓 、波動上升 ，並以
此前領漲多頭行情的晶片 、半導體和科技股
最明顯 。觀察今年以來 ，不止股市波動度上
升，債市的波動更不亞於 股票市場 ，統計過
去，當VIX恐慌指數與MOVE債券波動度走高
時，低波動或防禦股的表現 ，往往比成長股
更好，顯示當市場震盪加劇時 ，資金傾向轉
往穩健資產停泊。
► 最近一次聯準會升息 ，為2022 年因疫情爆
發、供應鏈中斷 ，導致通膨大幅上揚 ，迫使
央行採取緊縮貨幣政策 ，和目前通膨上升促
央行升息背景 類似。前次聯準會啟動升息後
數月，成長股 、費城半導體股表現受抑 ，防
禦股、價值股和低波動族群 ，在剛開始升息
的環境下 ，表現相對抗跌 。考量目前聯準 會
籠罩在升息陰霾下，可增加相關類股配置。
對等關稅宣布後，內需防禦股獲利下修幅度最少 最近一次聯準會升息後，防禦低波高息和價值股突出美國股債波動度上升時，低波動、防禦股表現較佳

金融市場波動上升，增配低波、防禦價值股更穩健

### Page 8

8
► 觀察最近一季 ，資訊科技 、通訊服務與非核
心消費等High Beta類股，表現明顯落後於大
盤；反觀價值股 、防禦股和低波動等因子 ，
甚至核 心消 費與 高 股利等 傳統類 防禦 性產
業，報酬相對較 穩健，受資金青睞 。顯示在
經歷過 上半 年科 技 、半導 體股的 大漲 行情
後，市場預期高 利率與地緣政治 風險可能維
持更久時間 ，重新聚焦在獲利持久、現金易
落袋為安的防禦型資產，更能持盈保泰。
► 從預估本 益比看，目前金融僅 16倍、防禦股
17.5倍、價值股 (以IWD ETF代表)17.9倍、
低波動 (以USMV ETF代表)19.6倍，皆低於
標普500指數 現在 的21.3倍，近月資金流向
物美價廉的低波動價值 、防禦族群，評價越
低、漲幅越高 。展望後市，AI長線基本面仍
持續看好 ，股市回檔或為評價 修正而 非盈餘
下滑，市場波動度提升之際 ，建議不過度 集
中於單一 產業或 個股，多元配置適度增加 低
波動 、 防禦 和價 值股，分 批布局 以控管 風

險，料將更能在震盪加劇時安穩求利。

### Page 9

股市
 美、歐、日央行升息抑通膨 ，利率創高添股市波動風險 ，加上成熟國家債務攀升 ，科技巨擘亦舉債
投入資本支出 ，未來獲利與變現 能力持續被市場檢視 ，金融市場震盪不減 。美伊衝突升溫，近期可
透過防禦、低波動或價值 股來降低風險；科技 及AI半導體等發展趨勢不變 ，獲利基本面佳 ，長線發
展可期，宜採逢回分批布局方式，分散國家與產業配置。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，全球利率彈升有利銀行股 ，歐、日銀行股基本面佳 ，歐洲國防軍工
仍具投資吸引力。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，中長
期看好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分
可關注金融等產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升，應避免超長天期債券投資 ，預期年底前美國 10年期公債利率將於 4.5%-5.0%
震盪，可逢利率走升時，以中短天期債券進行鎖利領息 。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款
能力，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
 可以非美貨幣債券如歐元或澳幣計價投資級債券，分散單一貨幣風險。

債種：中天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金融、
科技、通訊及公用事業等
衛星配置：非美貨幣債券分散風險
匯市
 聯準會升息且態度偏鷹，美元高檔盤整。
 日本銀行9月升息後，年底前或明年初仍可能再度緊縮，日圓震盪加大偏升。
美元高檔整理，日圓震盪加大偏升
商品
 沙烏地阿拉伯關閉輸油管線 ，加上荷姆茲海峽局勢緊張 ，能源供需失衡 ，油價年底前維持 偏高水位。
 短期受美國雙率走高有壓 ，惟美日財政赤字壓力 與去美元化使全球央行買盤依舊 ，提供黃金中長期
穩定支撐。

### Page 16

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

## Document 356: Q3 China GDP above expectations, but downside risks remain (2026-10-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Q3 China GDP above expectations, but downside risks remain (2026-10-23).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#china #asia #aud
Q3 China GDP above expectations, but downside risks remain
By Elliot Clarke
Head of International Economics, Westpac
16:03 October 19 2026 Share
Stimulus is required if success in 2026 is to be sustained through 2027 and 2028.
China GDP surprised to the upside in Q3 2026, quarterly growth accelerating from 0.9% in Q2 to 1.2%
in Q3. Annual growth lifted from 4.3% to 4.5% as a result; though, on a year-to-date basis, growth
edged down 0.1ppts to 4.6%. The Q3 result keeps authorities on track to achieve their 4.5-5.0%ytd

ambition for 2026, and also provides a little more time to accelerate domestic demand into 2027.

### Page 2

Broad-based stimulus is required because net exports’ contribution to growth will likely abate in coming
quarters now that the trade surplus is back at record highs, and as many Chinese firms are
increasingly splitting their focus between expanding domestic capacity and offshore production
networks.

While annual retail sales growth accelerated from 0.4% to 1.1% in September, the Q3 year-to-date
growth pulse of 1.1% is just a quarter of the gain reported a year ago. Automobiles, construction
materials and jewellery have been particular heavy detractors over the past year, but most of the other
sub-categories also lack momentum. As we detailed in our September Market Outlook, this is not
because households are cash constrained, but rather as sentiment is extremely weak and the outlook
for employment and wealth highly uncertain. Key for wealth is that house prices for new and
established homes continue to decline month-on-month (respectively 0.1% and 0.3% in September)
five years into the downturn. Property investment’s ongoing decline (-20.6%ytd) suggests this
persistent weakness in sales and prices is demand driven.

While total fixed asset investment has been an outright negative for annual GDP growth since April, a
recovery is not beyond either the public or private sector’s capacity. Rather, across the economy,
entities are waiting for direction and incentive from the Central Government. This is likely to come by
early-2027 to offset the loss of support from trade, and as downside risks to global growth become
more apparent. Helpfully, industrial production continues to grow at a robust pace, 5.3%ytd, borrowing
rates for businesses and households are low, and authorities are beginning to provide banks with
additional lending capacity. For larger businesses, anecdotes suggest officials are also encouraging
major institutional investors to increase their structural allocation to domestic equities, building lasting 
appetite for new equity issuance, which in turn will fund future waves of real economic capacity and
income generation.

We remain comfortable with the view that Chinese GDP growth will hold at or slightly above 4.5%
through 2026–2028. But, the longer the Central Government waits to trigger new real investment
across both the private and public sectors, the higher the likelihood of poor sentiment becoming
entrenched and downside risks crystallising. If authorities wait until after net exports’ contribution has
faded, even holding annual aggregate growth above 4.0% could become a significant challenge. For
this reason, we expect material stimulus initiatives to be announced in the next 3 to 6 months to reset
the property sector and turn the business investment trend.

## Document 359: Rates Spark_ US and France like ships passing in the night at 5%

Source PDF: all report pdf/Rates Spark_ US and France like ships passing in the night at 5%.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The US 10yr yield gapped back below 5% with some conviction.
We're not convinced it keeps on going. More likely it ultimately
reverts up again. Brent oil pushed above $110 for the ﬁrst time
since May, but weaker market sentiment prevented a hawkish
repricing. French bond spreads continue to surge, and we
struggle to see catalysts to push against this

Padhraic Garvey, CFA, Michiel Tukker and Benjamin Schroeder
10-year French government bonds are now yielding above 5%, whereas the
US 10-year Treasury has dipped below 5%
The US 10yr back below 5% - let's see how
it feels down there
The US 10yr yield broke back below 5% around midday in New York
trading on Thursday. And did so with a degree of conviction. We
thought 5% could hold, but always acknowledged that a
heightened (and still heightening) risk-oﬀ tone could in the end
dominate, at least for the near term. The fact that the French 10yr
has gone the other way and shot above 5% in recent days, and the
spread out to 175bp vs Germany, is also of relevance. We also note

### Page 2

that Thursday was the ﬁrst day of pronounced ﬂight into Bunds
since the French wobbles took hold. Treasuries have shared in this.
Whether we remain below 5% is a whole other question. We're
minded to expect it to snap back above, as we still have inﬂation /
ﬁscal deﬁcit / AI boom underpinnings to navigate. And we have the
US mid-term elections approaching as an excuse for trouble. But it
will need some degree of calm in the risk space ﬁrst. What happens

on Friday is anyone's guess, and it may well be that longs in cash
bond duration oﬀer comfort to most through an uncertain
weekend. We'll see. We still think the 10yr yield gets back above 5%
ultimately. The timing for that is a tougher call.
It's also true that there has been a tendency shown in recent weeks
to buy into concessional 5+% levels for the US 10yr. We saw that in
spades post the recent poor CPI number, which was followed by an
immediate sell-oﬀ in Treasuries, but then an even bigger

subsequent rally. In a way, the latest break below 5% is a
culmination of that move. Now that 5% has been breached below,
where next?
More pertinently, can the market ﬁnd comfort at sub 5% levels
given the contemporaneous inﬂation dynamic. And, can the
market really ignore the fact that neither side has a plan for the
ﬁscal deﬁcit as we roll into the mid-term elections in the next
couple of weeks. One thing is for sure: the risk-oﬀ environment
takes pressure oﬀ the Fed to hike next week. But even given that,

we'd not be loading up on duration here, at least not just yet.
French downgrade lurking and risk
sentiment not helping French spreads either
Amid all this, French bonds remain in the line of ﬁre with the spread
over Bunds widening at times by 15bp, with the spread touching
177bp. That is still below its late-2011 peak of just under 200bp,
but at this pace it is only a matter of days before we see a new
high. Although a lot has changed in the eurozone since 2011, some

may consider Italy’s spreads during the sovereign debt crisis the
better yardstick since it was the country in market focus back then
and not France.

That said, the latest widening seems to be more driven by the
deteriorating general sentiment, as there were no signiﬁcant news
headlines out of France. But the prospect of ongoing uncertainty
until the presidential elections keeps investors sidelined. And French
bonds can’t really beneﬁt from any recovery in general sentiment,
leaving spreads at wider levels. For now, it feels like watching a train
wreck in slow motion where we have not seen the end of the

widening yet.
One event that market observers have been looking forward to is
the scheduled rating review by Moody’s after markets close on
Friday. Moody’s still has France on Aa3 with a negative outlook, and
therefore one notch above the other two major rating agencies that
place France already at A+, even with stable outlooks for France. A
downgrade from Moody’s now looks overdue and should be
considered a catch-up. Commentary will be parsed for further

downgrade triggers, since the developments in France – in terms of
political stability, public resistance and reﬁnancing costs – oﬀer
little hope of improvement anytime soon. In terms of timing, note
that there is no ﬁxed release time. When Moody’s downgraded
France in 2024, the news hit the wires only just after midnight CET.
Having at least this one risk event out of the way might bring about
some stabilisation. But any lasting relief would need positive news.

A budget for 2027 with at least some semblance of ﬁscal
consolidation would be a start, but might also have to wait until
December.
Friday’s events and market view
PMI data on Friday will be the next test for markets. So far,
both equity markets and economic data point to a resilient
economic outlook, but this narrative is challenged by rising
rates and higher oil prices. Concerns about AI valuations are
a recurring daily theme while energy prices keep drifting

higher. If we were to also see headwinds materialising in the
economic data, we could face quite a bullish repricing in
rates. Especially for euro rates, a more downbeat growth

## Document 402: US MARKET CALL_ Yes, AI Is Too Big To Fail

Source PDF: all report pdf/US MARKET CALL_ Yes, AI Is Too Big To Fail.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-17
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: US MARKET CALL: Yes, AI Is Too Big To Fail
Date:    3 hours ago
US MARKET CALL: Yes, AI Is Too Big To Fail

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
US MARKET CALL: Yes, AI Is
Too Big To Fail
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 17 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Nils Rasmusson / Unsplash
I. Bonds
We've all been suffering from AI fatigue for a while. The daily news about it
has been head-spinning. We've been warned that AI is a bubble that will
burst, just as the late-1990s tech bubble led to the Tech Wreck in the early
2000s. Analogies to the credit excesses of the Great Financial Crisis have

### Page 3

also been proffered. Last week's rout in AI-related stocks convinced the
naysayers that the AI bubble is bursting, just as they’ve long predicted it
would.
Our happier spin is that the Bond Vigilantes are now doing a good job of
regulating and allocating capital to the AI ecosystem. Credit-quality
spreads are widening to reflect the riskiness of the bonds issued by some
of the dodgier AI-related borrowers like CoreWeave and Oracle. The
rapidly rising cost of credit for such borrowers should deflate the AI bubble

without bursting it. If some AI-related credits do blow up, the Godfathers of
the AI mafia (along with their protectors in the government) will clean up
the mess. As in "The Godfather II," Michael Corleone has too much at
stake to let his brother Fredo screw up the family business.
Meanwhile, the rout in the AI corporate bond market has triggered a flight
to quality and safety that has stopped the rout in the US Treasury bond
market (chart). This confirms our thesis that the recent rise in US bond

yields mostly reflects better-than-expected economic growth. Tougher
credit conditions in the AI corporate bond market should curb some of the
AI excesses. However, we doubt this will trigger an economy-wide credit
crunch or a recession. We aren't convinced that it will slow capital
spending much, since labor and materials shortages, along with public
NIMBY pushback, have already slowed the pace of data center
construction.
II. S&P Earnings

### Page 4

The big banks reported mixed but solid overall Q3 earnings last week. On
average, they met banking industry analysts' expectations. Last week, the
consensus EPS for all analysts covering S&P 500 companies edged up to
$365.70 this year, $424.19 next year, and $492.62 in 2028 (chart). Those
are extremely bullish expectations considering that we are still forecasting
$500 in 2030! If the analysts are right, the S&P 500 could rise to 10,000
by the end of next year, assuming a forward P/E of 20.0-21.0. For now, we

are sticking with 10,000 by the end of the Roaring 2020s, i.e., at the end
of 2029.
Last week, Q3's consensus EPS growth rate remained at 24.0% y/y, while
the Q4 estimate rose to 29.8% (chart). We've been among the most
bullish investment strategists on the Street, but analysts are still ahead of
us.

### Page 5

The S&P 500, S&P 400, and S&P 600 continued to rise to record highs
last week (chart).
III. Stock Valuation
The recent stock market selloff has been driven by falling forward P/Es
even as forward earnings have been rising to record highs (chart). The
former are nearing the troughs they made during last year's "Liberation
Day" lows.
Since roughly mid-2025, rising FEMO (fabulous earnings momentum) has
more than offset falling FOMO (fear of missing out) (chart). Since October

6, the reverse has been true: Falling FOMO is outpacing the rise in
FEMO.

### Page 6

IV. Performance
The stock market had a tough week last week. Leading the 1.95%
pullback was MAGS (down 3.89%), while XMAGS fell 1.38% (chart). The
Russell 2000 fell 2.4%, while the Nasdaq fell 4.5%. The DJIA rose 0.67%
for the week. The Impressive 493 is still outperforming the Magnificent-7
on a year-to-date basis, with a gain of 14.1% versus 10.2%.
V. Sentiment
The market letter writers have been more bullish than retail investors
lately (chart). Odds are they will both be a bit more pessimistic this week.

### Page 7

Only 47.4% of the S&P 500 are trading above their 200-day moving
averages (chart).
VI. Commodities
Aluminum prices are falling after matching their 2022 peak. This suggests
that supply-chain disruptions attributable to Gulf War III are receding, as
they had done after Russia invaded Ukraine (chart).

### Page 8

Copper prices remained near their recent record high last week despite
chatter that tightening credit conditions could slow the AI buildout and
weigh on overall GDP growth (chart).
The outlook for higher-for-longer diesel prices offers no relief (chart).

### Page 9

We aren't giving up on gold yet. The current correction resembles
previous ones that occurred in the middle of significant gold bull runs
(chart). This time, rising interest rates have been weighing on the metal's
price. However, significant buying by the People's Bank of China and
other central banks isn't interest-rate sensitive. We are still aiming for a
gold price of $5,000 an ounce by the end of this year or early next year.
💡

log in to the Yardeni QuickTakes website and post them at the end
of the QuickTakes article. Paid members' contributions may be
featured in our segment, "Ed Answers Your Questions".

### Page 2

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts 
are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
No surprise in the October CPI
The new complete monthly CPI gained 3.8% in the year to 
October, coming in a touch softer than Westpac’s estimate of 
3.9%yr but a bit stronger than the market estimate of 3.6%yr. 
So while the market and other commentators may have been 
surprised by the result it was broadly within our expectations 
and as such has had just a marginal impact on our inflation 
profile.
The October CPI was flat in the month, a touch softer than 
Westpac’s published near-cast of 0.1% on the back of a larger 
than expected decline in electricity (–10.2%) which was only 
partially offset by a smaller than expected decline in auto fuel 
(–0.9%) and an unexpected bump up in garments (+2.6%).
The Trimmed Mean (TM) lifted 0.3% in the month of October, 
the same monthly increase it has seen for the previous two 
months and down from the 0.5%mth increase in July but 
stronger than the 0.2%mth prints from March to June.
This saw the annual pace of the TM increase to 3.3% from 
3.2% in September. This month the TM excluded the annual 
rise in electricity; alongside other large price rises and falls. 
Due to the short history of many series in the monthly CPI, the 
annual pace of monthly TM inflation can only be calculated 
back to April 2025. Before then the ABS notes that the annual 
pace is calculated from the quarterly CPI data.
For more information on the October monthly CPI see 
“Electrical noise is far from static ”.
Monthly CPI points to some momentum
Following the October CPI we have seen some minor revisions 
to the various components in our December quarter CPI 
estimate with the upward revisions, such as transport via auto 
fuel and motor vehicles, being offset by downward revisions 
to recreation, alcohol & tobacco and utilities in particular. This 
has left our December CPI estimate unchanged at 0.6%qtr.   
As our Chief Economist, Luci Ellis, has noted that inflation 
did see a bump in the September quarter and again in the 
month of October. However, the main sources of the surprise 
have had little to do with domestic demand or labour market 
pressures. Rather, a sizeable part of the bump looks to have 
been from administered prices and noise. Since the release of 
the data, the RBA has recognised this issue but at the post 
RBA Board Decision Media Conference in December, the 
Governor communicated that the Bank is more worried about 
upside risks to inflation than first thought.
We agree that some of this momentum will continue into the 
December quarter as highlighted by our upward revision in our 
We will continue to focus on the quarterly prints
Justin Smirk
Senior Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
December quarter TM estimate to 0.8%qtr/3.2%yr from our 
earlier estimate of 0.7%qtr/3.1%yr. 
For the December quarter, price changes that are included in 
the TM go from a low of –0.26% to a high of 1.66%. Readers 
will remember that the TM is calculated from seasonally 
adjusted price changes and not the original estimates so if 
unaffected by regular seasonal price shifts. We estimate 
that the most significant components to be trimmed off 
the top in the quarter are: electricity (2.7%); other services 
for motor vehicles (2.5%); tobacco (2.1%); and automotive 
fuel (1.7%). Trimmed off the bottom are: other non-durable 
household products (–0.39%); garments for women (–0.92%); 
international holiday travel (–0.97%); vegetables (–1.16%) and 
wine (–1.27%).   
However, we do not expect this momentum to continue 
through 2026. As Luci noted in her article “ RBA on hold 
in 2026, risks on both sides ” we expect inflation to keep 
easing, due to the lagged impact of past restrictive policy 
and the unwind of earlier government support measures. We 
believe that many commentators have overlooked these lags. 
Monetary policy typically takes a year or more to fully affect 
jobs and prices, so current outcomes reflect the peak cash rate 
from a year ago and we expect to see further labour market 
softening. Strong hiring across the care economy has masked 
some of that restrictiveness, as has public infrastructure 
spending, but these supports are now fading along with last 
year’s tax-cut boost. We believe that even with the 75bps in  
rate cuts this year, policy remains mildly restrictive, and given 
usual lags, this will keep showing in the data through 2026.
Taking a look at some of the recent larger moves in monthly 
prices can highlight many of the issues Luci raises. Taking the 
seasonally adjusted data, smoothing it with a three month 
average then calculating the change over three months (so we 
have three months on three months percentage change) we 
find at the top end of the list some price increases you would 
not expect to continue at the same pace. 
For example at the top are: lamb & goat (8.1%, highly 
seasonal); accessories (8.1% with jewellery prices surging 
on the back of record high gold prices); audio visual & 
computing services (6.0% due to Netflix subscriptions); beef 
& veal (3.7%, highly seasonal); coffee & tea (3.6% noting 
global coffee prices have passed their peak and are already 
significantly lower); water & sewerage (2.3% IPART decision 
was for a 13.8% increase for Sydney Water in October); 
property rates & charges (2.3% administered prices); and 
electricity (1.2%, reflecting unwinding of cost of living 
rebates). Offsetting some of these increases was falling prices 
for: men’s footwear (–2.0%); vegetables (–2.0% seasonal); 
eggs (–1.5%, reflecting the unwinding of the price spike 
associated with an outbreak of bird flu); and major household 
appliances (–1.2%). 
Core inflation still to moderate through 2026
We are projecting headline inflation to get back to the RBA’s 
target band, but not until later in 2026. We now see core 
inflation back below the midpoint of the 2–3% range at the 
end of the year. We had thought this might be achieved by the 
third quarter but now expect current momentum to continue

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
for a bit longer. However, it is worth noting that our estimate
of the six month annualised pace of the TM is down to 2.4%yr
by June 2026.
Electricity to boost November CPI
Westpac is estimating a 0.4% increase in the November

monthly CPI which will hold the annual pace flat at 3.8%yr.
The seasonally adjusted estimate is 0.5% in the month
suggesting it is a stronger than usual estimate.
Boosting the November estimate is: a 16% surge in electricity
(as the impact of the last round of rebates fades); a 3.2% jump
in gas & other household fuels; and a 2.3% bump in auto fuel
(based on reported pump prices). Partially offsetting these
gains is a –2.6% decline in holiday travel & accommodation

and a –2.9% drop in clothing & footwear.
Take care with the Monthly CPI
Many series in the monthly CPI only date back to early 2024
so seasonal adjustment is somewhat problematic. Some
series did have a longer monthly history coming from the
previously published monthly CPI indicator and it appears
the ABS may have used some of this to seasonally adjust the
data. However, we caution that some of the new data has a
different history to the old data and as such, we expect it is

going to take some time to understand the seasonal behaviour
of the new data.
For example, for male garments there is some difference
with the reported monthly change compared to the old CPI
Indicator series but this appears to be only marginal. However,
for women’s, the difference can be significant with even a
reversal of the sign of change in some recent months. And for
some series with a short history and no monthly indicator data
to compare to some of the adjustment appears to be a little

arbitrary, as you can see in the chart right which compares the
children’s garments index in original and seasonally adjusted
terms. The only adjustment made to the series was in June
2025 when a –3.2% change was adjusted to a flat print. Why?
We are yet to uncover.
You can also see where the ABS has turned to smoothing
processes to seasonally adjust short data series. In the chart
over the seasonally adjusted series looks as if has been
generated by applying a Henderson Trend to the original

children’s footwear series.
As such, we expect it is possible there will be significant
adjustments made to the seasonally adjustment process as
data is collected which could result in significant revisions to
seasonally adjusted series including the Trimmed Mean and
the Weighted Median.

## Document 479: スペイン、前倒しの総選挙へ ～政権交代なら住宅・移民政策を軌道修正～

Source PDF: all report pdf/スペイン、前倒しの総選挙へ ～政権交代なら住宅・移民政策を軌道修正～.pdf

Europe Trends

2026 年 10 月６日

1/3 
スペイン、前倒しの総選挙へ 
～政権交代なら住宅・移民政策を軌道修正～ 
 経済調査部 首席エコノミスト  田中 理 
（℡：050-5474-7494）

（要旨） 
○ スペインのサンチェス首相は10 月５日、 議会を解散し、11 月 29 日に前倒し総選挙を実施す
ると発表した。少数政権による議会運営は予算や住宅政策を巡って行き詰まり、カタルーニ
ャ独立派政党との関係悪化も政権運営を難しくしていた。足元の世論調査では中道右派の国
民党（ＰＰ）が第１党、右派ポピュリストのボックス（Vox）が第３党につけ、ＰＰ単独では
なくVox の協力を前提とした政権発足の可能性が高い。 政権交代となれば、 住宅政策は規制 ・
借家人保護重視から供給拡大や規制緩和へ軸足を移し、 移民政策も厳格化する可能性がある。
特に Vox の影響力が強まれば、人口・労働力の増加に支えられてきたスペインの成長モデル
や、ＥＵ・カタルーニャとの関係にも変化が及ぶ可能性がある。

■少数政権の行き詰まり、住宅問題が解散の引き金に 
スペインのサンチェス首相は10 月５日、議会を解散し、11 月 29 日に前倒し総選挙を実施する
と発表した。2027 年までの議会任期を全うするとしてきた従来の方針を転換したもので、2023 年
の総選挙後に続いてきた少数政権による不安定な議会運営はひとまず終止符を打つことになる。
今回の解散・総選挙は、少数与党による議会運営の行き詰まりに、与党関係者を巡る様々な疑
惑、カタルーニャ独立派の地域政党との関係悪化、住宅危機への国民の不満などが重なり、政府
が重要政策を成立させることが難しくなったことが背景にある。 
2023 年の総選挙後に発足したサンチェス政権は、中道左派の「社会労働党（PSOE）」と左派の
「スマール（Sumar）」による連立政権だが、両党だけでは下院の過半数を確保できない。そのた
め、政権発足時には、「カタルーニャ共和主義左翼（ＥＲＣ）」、「ともにカタルーニャのために
（Junts）」、「バスク民族党（ＰＮＶ）」、「バスク統一党（Bildu）」など、カタルーニャやバスクを
中心とする地域政党から協力を取り付けた。なかでも、サンチェス政権の存続に重要な役割を果
たしてきたのがJuntsである。2023 年の政権発足時には、2017 年のカタルーニャの独立運動を巡
って有罪判決を受けた政治家や活動家を対象とする恩赦を条件に、サンチェス首相の続投に協力
したが、連立政権には参加せず、その後も個々の法案毎に賛否を判断してきた。このため、少数
の議席を持つ地域政党が政府の法案成立を左右する不安定な状況が続いてきた。 
政権運営の機能不全を象徴しているのが予算だ。政府は2023 年予算を最後に新たな予算を成立

### Page 2

2/3 
させることができず、過去３年は既存予算の延長を余儀なくされてきた。2026 年７月には、2027
年予算編成の前提となる財政安定化計画も、中道右派の「国民党（ＰＰ） 」、右派ポピュリストの
「ボックス（Vox）」、Juntsなどの反対で議会で否決された。閣僚経験者、党幹部、首相の家族な
ど、与党関係者を巡る疑惑が相次いで表面化したことも、政権への風当たりを強めた。それで
も、サンチェス首相は2027 年までの議会任期を全うする方針を維持してきた。 
こうした状況を大きく変えたのが住宅問題だ。スペインでは人口や世帯数の増加に住宅供給が
追いつかず、住宅価格と家賃の上昇が大きな社会問題となっている。９月下旬にマドリードで87
歳の女性が長年暮らしてきた住居から立ち退かされたことを契機に抗議活動が広がり、住宅問題
が一気に政治争点として浮上した（詳細は10 月１日付けレポート「スペイン高成長の裏で深まる
住宅危機」を参照されたい）。政府は立ち退き停止措置の延長や賃貸市場の規制強化などを盛り込
んだ緊急政令を議会に提出したが、ＰＰやVoxなどの野党勢力に加えて、Juntsも反対に回った結
果、承認されなかった。Juntsは借家人保護や賃貸規制の強化が住宅所有者を賃貸市場から退出さ
せ、結果的に供給不足や家賃上昇を深刻化させると主張した。サンチェス首相が総選挙の前倒し
を決断した背景には、こうした議会運営の行き詰まりがある。同時に、住宅危機への国民の関心
が急速に高まるなか、住宅政策を選挙の中心的な争点に据えることで、劣勢の挽回を図る狙いも
あるとみられる。

■政権交代の可能性が高い、Voxの政策関与に注目 
 各種の世論調査では、ＰＰが30％台前半の支持を集めてリードし、それを20％台後半のPSOEが
追い、Voxが20％近くで三番手につけている（図）。PSOEが現政権で連立を組むSumarは５～６％前
後で低迷している。前倒し総選挙の正式発表後に実施された世論調査はまだ公表されていない。
このまま世論調査に近い結果となれば、PSOEがＰＰに政権を明け渡すことになりそうだ。だが、
ＰＰも単独で過半数を確保するのは難しく、安定政権の樹立にはVoxの協力が必要となる公算が大
きい。Voxが連立政権の一員に加わるのか、それとも閣外からＰＰ政権を支えるのかによって、政
策決定への関与の度合いが変わってくる。既に複数の州政府で、ＰＰが主導する政権にVoxが連立
パートナーとして参加している。連立政権に加わる場合、ＰＰのフェイホー党首が首相に就任
し、Voxが副首相や閣僚ポストを獲得する形が考えられる。閣外協力にとどまる場合、政権発足時
や重要法案の審議でＰＰ政権を支えることになる。 
ＰＰが主導する政権が誕生した場合、PSOE政権の公的介入路線が軌道修正され、減税や規制緩
和を通じた経済活性化を目指す可能性が高い。ＰＰのフェイホー党首は、年間20 万戸規模の住宅
建設、土地供給の拡大、建設手続きの簡素化、住宅関連税制の軽減などを掲げている。移民政策
の転換も予想される。サンチェス政権は移民の受け入れに比較的寛容で、移民による人口と労働
力の増加は近年のスペインの高成長を支える重要な要因となってきた。移民規制が強まれば、住
宅需要への圧力を幾分弱める可能性がある反面、労働供給の増加ペースが鈍化する。観光、建
設、農業、介護など移民労働力への依存度が高い業種では人手不足や賃金上昇圧力が強まること
が予想される。特にVoxは、不法移民の強制送還、移民への給付制限、長期滞在資格の厳格化な

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
ど、移民政策の強化を主張している。ＰＰとVoxが連立を組む州政府では、住宅や公共サービスで
スペイン国民を優先する考えが盛り込まれた例もある。 
 ＰＰが主導する政権の誕生は、カタルーニャ独立派との対話路線が転換され、独立の火種を抱
えるカタルーニャ州政府との関係を再び悪化させる恐れがある。ＰＰがかつて率いた政権では、
領土的一体性や法の執行を重視し、カタルーニャの独立問題に厳しく対処した。次期政権とカタ
ルーニャ州政府との緊張が再び高まれば、金融市場でスペインの政治リスクが意識される可能性
がある。 
 Voxの政権参加によるＥＵ関係の悪化を懸念する声もあるが、ＰＰ自体は長年スペインの政権を
率いてきた親ＥＵの中道右派政党で、政権交代によってスペインのＥＵ政策が大幅に変わること
は想定しにくい。ただ、Voxの政策面での影響力が増すことで、移民政策、気候変動政策、農業政
策などでは、スペイン政府とＥＵ機関との摩擦が増える可能性がある。 
現時点でスペインの政治リスクをフランスと同列に扱う必要はない。スペイン経済は相対的に
高い成長を維持しており、今回の解散も財政危機や景気後退を背景としたものではない。むし
ろ、市場にとって重要なのは、総選挙によってこれまでの議会の機能不全が解消され、安定した
政権が成立するかどうかである。次期政権が安定多数を確保すれば、予算を成立させられなかっ
た現政権に比べ、短期的には政策の予見可能性が改善する。一方で、Voxの影響力が大きくなるほ
ど、移民政策、気候変動対策、カタルーニャ政策、ＥＵ政策での変化が大きくなる。特に移民政
策の厳格化は、人口増加に支えられてきたスペインの成長モデルに中期的な変化をもたらす。

## Document 519: 住宅価格高騰と女性のキャリア形成 ～子育て世帯の家探し、近さをとるか、広さをとるか～

Source PDF: all report pdf/住宅価格高騰と女性のキャリア形成 ～子育て世帯の家探し、近さをとるか、広さをとるか～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Research Report

2026 年 10 月 06 日

1/10 
住宅価格高騰と女性のキャリア形成 
～子育て世帯の家探し、近さをとるか、広さをとるか～ 
資産運用調査部 福澤 涼子

（要旨） 
○ 住宅価格の高騰に伴い、「夫婦ともに正社員として働く子育て世帯」の家探しが難しくなっ
ている。夫婦双方が正社員で就業しながら、保育所や学童保育への送迎時間に間に合うため
には、「職住近接」が家探しのひとつの重要な基準となるためである。 
○ しかし、東京 23 区の住宅価格はここ数年で高騰している。東京 23 区のファミリー向けの広
さ(70㎡)のマンション平均価格は、中古でも1億 2千万円を超え、賃貸住宅でも平均月額賃
料が 25 万円に達している。経済的に都心部に住むことを断念する子育て世帯も増えていく
だろう。 
○ 住居の郊外化でキャリアが変わるのは、特に子育て期の女性に見られる傾向である。未就学
児を育てる女性の場合、東京 23 区や隣接エリアに住む女性のほうが、そうでないエリアより
も正社員での就業率が高い傾向にあった。正社員としてキャリアを維持し続けるには、都心
部などの豊富な労働市場へのアクセスを維持することが重要である。 
○ 他方で、東京23 区や隣接エリアに住む子育て世帯は、豊かな住生活を実現するための「誘導
居住面積水準」を達成している世帯の割合が、非隣接エリアよりも低い。住宅価格の高騰が
長期化すれば、住宅の狭小化がさらに進むと考えられる。 
○ 住宅価格高騰を女性活躍の壁としないためには、「職場への近接性」と「子育てに必要な広
さ」のいずれかを大きく犠牲にしなくてよい居住環境を整える必要がある。そのためには、
国や大都市圏の自治体が子育て世帯向けの賃貸住宅を供給したり、既存住宅の世代間循環を
促す仕組みなどについて実現可能性や課題を検討していく必要がある。

1．子育てしながら正社員で働く女性の増加と、住居探しの難しさ 
住宅価格の高騰に伴い、家探しに苦労している人も多い。とりわけ難しい状況を強いられてい
るのが、家探しに制約の多い「夫婦ともに正社員などとしてフルタイムで働く子育て世帯」であ
る。その制約のひとつは「通勤にかけられる時間」であり、夫婦ともにキャリアと子育てを両立
するためには、夫婦双方が保育所や学童保育所への送迎に関わることが望ましく、居住地として
選択できるエリアは自ずと限られてくる。特に、労働需要の高い都心部への近接性は、多くの子
育て中の就業者にとって家探しの重要な視点である。

### Page 2

2/10 
かつて標準的であった「正社員の夫と専業主婦の妻」からなる世帯では、子どもの送迎をはじ
めとする子育ての多くを妻が担っていたため、夫の通勤時間が長くても、日々の生活は成り立っ
ていた。また、共働きであっても、 「正社員の夫とパートタイムで働く妻」という形態では、妻が
転居先の近くで短時間の仕事を探すことで、その暮らしが維持できた。妻の働き方を制限するこ
とで、広さを求めて郊外に住居を買うという選択肢をもつことができていたといえる。しかし、
夫婦ともに正社員などのフルタイムとして働く世帯の場合、夫婦ともに職住近接を選択すること
が仕事と子育てを両立するうえでの基盤となる。資料 1に示したとおり、そのような小さい子ど
もを育てながら正規職員として就業する女性は増加傾向にあり、2022年には専業主婦やパート雇
用を抜いて最も多い割合となった。

資料 1  夫婦と子ども（末子の年齢が 8 歳以下）から成る世帯の妻の就業状況・雇用形態

（注）元データの制約により、妻の就業状況について、「会社などの役員」を除いて構成比を算出。 
（出所）総務省の各年の「就業構造基本調査 全国編 世帯単位で見た統計表」をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所が作成

なお、オンラインの活用によるリモート勤務の普及によって、勤務地から離れた地域に住むと
いう選択肢も取りやすくなった。しかし近年は、企業において出社日数を増やす動きもみられ
（注 1） 、在宅勤務を前提とした居住地選択はリスクとなりつつある。そのため、子育て世帯にと
って、勤務地へのアクセスは引き続き重要な住居選択の条件である。夫婦ともに正社員として働
く世帯の場合、保育所の利用時間のなかに、夫婦の通勤、勤務、休憩、送迎にかかる時間を収め
る必要がある。そのため、まずは勤務地からの距離を基準に、家探しをすることになり、さら
に、子どもの人数に応じた部屋の数や防音設備、近隣の保育所や学童保育所の空き状況なども考
慮すると、選択肢がさらに狭まってくる。

2．都心部の住宅価格の高騰で、子育て世帯に重い負担

さらには、昨今の住宅価格高騰が、職場へのアクセスを重視する夫婦にどこに自宅を構えるべ
きかという難題をつきつける。例えば、「ファミリー向け」と称される70㎡換算の中古マンショ
ン住宅価格は、2024年以降急激に上昇している（資料 2）。もちろん、都心3 区などの富裕層向け
物件が平均値を押し上げているものの、比較的買いやすいとされてきた城東地区や城北地区で
